## Phase 1 Isolation Notes (Current)
- `LinkedSpec` now avoids eager plugin dependency at module load:
  - Removed top-level `use PPlugin;`.
  - `AUTOLOAD` performs lazy `require PPlugin` only when plugin execution is requested.
- `LinkedSpec::get_parser` now prefers module-relative spec resolution:
  - resolves to `../specs/<name>.spec` relative to `perl/LinkedSpec.pm` location,
  - avoids hard dependence on current working directory,
  - keeps `PathSearch` as lazy fallback only.
- `LinkedSpec::get_parser` now validates spec-name input early:
  - `undef`/empty/whitespace-only/non-scalar/padded/control-byte spec-name arguments fail fast with diagnostics and `undef` return,
  - invalid-name calls do not trigger fallback loader paths.
- `LinkedSpec::get_parser` now treats unresolved path-like names as explicit misses:
  - when local/module-relative lookup fails for path-like names, it returns not-found directly,
  - this avoids unnecessary `PathSearch` fallback load attempts for explicit-path errors.
- `LinkedSpec::get_parser` now treats unresolved `.spec` basenames as explicit misses:
  - when local/module-relative lookup fails for `.spec`-suffixed names, it returns not-found directly,
  - this avoids `PathSearch` fallback attempts that would otherwise probe `<name>.spec.spec`.
- `LinkedSpec::get_parser` now traps `PathSearch->go` runtime exceptions:
  - fallback runtime failures are converted to diagnostics + `undef` return,
  - explicit `die` from `PathSearch` no longer escapes from `get_parser`.
- `LinkedSpec::get_parser` fallback now uses a single guarded resolver call:
  - duplicate unguarded `PathSearch->go` invocation removed,
  - fallback resolution is now fully covered by the eval-guarded call path.
- `LinkedSpec::get_parser` now distinguishes missing paths vs directory paths:
  - unresolved explicit path-like names report `Spec path not found`,
  - resolved directory and non-regular paths report `Spec path is not a file`.
- Regression harness decoupled from direct `Lispish.pm` import:
  - uses LinkedSpec-generated `Lispish` parser coderef for stream parsing in corpus tests.
- Fallback-path coupling cleanup:
  - `PathSearch.pm` no longer imports `Global.pm` (unused for `PathSearch->go` behavior).
  - This prevents fallback-only parser resolution from loading `HUtils`/`Lispish` through `Global`.
- Rule-compilation structure now exposes deterministic execution metadata:
  - `LinkedSpec::Get(..., return_descriptor => 1)` returns generated descriptor internals for tooling (`spec` + `gdata`),
  - `spec_entry(...)` now records `meta` per rule (node type, counts, execution shape, selected handler variant),
  - handler-template selection now follows deterministic metadata-based routing instead of hash-key iteration order.
- AND action semantics are now structurally explicit in template selection:
  - one regex/action edge uses `AND_SINGLE_ACODE`,
  - multi-regex/action AND continues to use `AND_ACODE` loop template.
- Bootstrap grammar hardcode is now explicitly indexed by rule identity:
  - each bootstrap rule carries stable `id` + semantic `tags`,
  - `startREs` and dispatch routing are built from tag-driven registry data,
  - recursive brace handler dispatch now resolves `CURLY_BRACE` by ID rather than fixed index constants.
- `spec_entry()` now follows an explicit staged RuleIR pipeline:
  - collect phase: parsed entries are normalized into RuleIR (`_collect_rule_ir`),
  - plan phase: execution metadata is derived from RuleIR (`_plan_rule_ir_meta`),
  - validate phase: incompatible action-mode mixes are rejected (`_validate_rule_ir_or_exit`),
  - emit-context phase: code chunks and action mappings are normalized for handler assembly (`_build_rule_ir_emit_context`).
- Runtime action rewriting now flows through `_rewrite_action_code_with_diagnostics(...)`:
  - helper events are scanned and promoted into canonical action-IR,
  - lowering is applied from canonical events via `_lower_action_code_from_canonical_ir(...)`,
  - `call_spec_handler_subst()` is retained as a compatibility/test shim and is not the runtime emission entrypoint.
- Action rewriter diagnostics are now first-class metadata in RuleIR emit flow:
  - unresolved helper forms are detected by `_find_unresolved_action_helpers(...)`,
  - diagnostics are accumulated across ACODE/BCODE/lifecycle chunks and exposed at `spec->{rule}{meta}{action_rewriter}`,
  - current diagnostics scope focuses on helper-surface mismatch detection without changing rewrite/runtime behavior.
- Action helper lowering contracts are now explicit and shared across rewrite subsystems:
  - helper lowering is declared centrally by `_build_action_lowering_contracts($label)`,
  - rewrite rules are compiled from contracts (`_build_action_rewrite_rules`) rather than hardcoded duplication,
  - unresolved-helper diagnostics reuse contract-declared unresolved patterns and helper labels,
  - per-rule metadata now includes `action_rewriter.rewrite_contract_ids` to expose active lowering-contract surface.
- Helper action-IR node metadata is now collected pre-lowering and exposed for migration tooling:
  - lowering contracts now carry canonical `ir_node` identities,
  - `_collect_action_helper_ir_nodes(...)` records helper action-IR hits before textual lowering is applied,
  - aggregated IR-node counters are surfaced at `spec->{rule}{meta}{action_rewriter}` as `helper_action_ir_count`, `helper_action_ir_nodes`, and `helper_action_ir_hits`.
- Helper action-IR metadata now includes structured helper payload events:
  - helper invocations are scanned pre-lowering with `_scan_contract_ir_events(...)`,
  - payload argument strings are normalized with `_trim_action_ir_value(...)`,
  - `spec->{rule}{meta}{action_rewriter}{helper_action_ir_events}` now provides per-invocation payload records (`ir_node`, `contract_id`, `raw`, `args`) to support canonical action-IR promotion.
- Canonical action-IR promotion now builds statement-level lowering input metadata:
  - helper payload events are promoted by `_canonicalize_helper_action_ir_event(...)`,
  - `_build_canonical_action_ir_events(...)` merges helper-derived canonical events with `RAW_PERL` fallback events for non-helper statements,
  - canonical metadata surface now includes `canonical_action_ir_*` fields under `spec->{rule}{meta}{action_rewriter}` for direct lowering-boundary consumption.
- Canonical action-IR lowering is now active in rewrite execution flow:
  - `_lower_action_code_from_canonical_ir(...)` consumes canonical action-IR events to apply helper lowerings event-by-event,
  - lowering is now canonical-IR-first instead of whole-code regex-pass-first,
  - unresolved helper forms remain unmodified when contract lowering does not apply, preserving unresolved-helper diagnostics.
- Canonical action-IR statement splitting is now nesting-aware:
  - `_split_action_ir_statements(...)` now scans with depth/quote tracking over `()`, `{}`, `[]`, and quoted strings,
  - nested semicolons inside helper payloads no longer fragment helper statements into false `RAW_PERL` canonical fallback entries.
- Canonical helper lowering is now whitespace-tolerant:
  - helper substitution regexes in `_build_action_lowering_contracts(...)` now accept optional spacing around helper names, parentheses, and arguments,
  - spacing-only helper variants now lower through canonical action-IR flow instead of remaining unresolved.
- Canonical helper lowering now covers return-call wrappers:
  - `_build_action_lowering_contracts(...)` includes a `return_call` contract for full-statement `return call(Rule)` forms,
  - `_scan_contract_ir_events(...)` + `_canonicalize_helper_action_ir_event(...)` now carry return-call wrapper events into canonical CALL lowering.
- Canonical helper lowering now covers indexed push-call wrappers:
  - `_build_action_lowering_contracts(...)` includes `push_call_indexed_builtin` for `push @target, call(Rule)->[index]` forms,
  - non-indexed `push_call_builtin` now uses a boundary guard so indexed wrappers are routed deterministically to the indexed contract.
- Method-like parser entrypoints now support chained method blocks:
  - `_parse_method_call_chain(...)` and `_render_method_call_chain(...)` now normalize `.method(...).method2(...)` chains for both action and non-action method-like bootstrap handlers,
  - chained method parsing now accepts empty argument lists (`()`), preserving multi-step method-like authoring ergonomics.
- Canonical helper lowering now covers typed declaration methods:
  - `declare(type, ...)` with `type ∈ {array, scalar, hash}` is now lowered through dedicated `DECLARE` contracts and IR events,
  - declaration aliases (`declare_a/s/h` and `declare_array/scalar/hash`) map to the same typed declaration semantics.
- Method-style empty-action argument normalization is now whitespace-tolerant at outer boundaries:
  - `METHOD_EMPTY_ACTION_CODE_BLOCK` processing trims `^\s*\(` and `\)\s*$` around outer argument payloads,
  - leading-space argument forms no longer lose closing-balance alignment during helper rewrite/lowering paths.
- Canonical statement splitting is now line-comment-aware:
  - `_split_action_ir_statements(...)` now tracks Perl line comments outside quoted strings,
  - semicolons inside line comments are ignored by top-level statement splitting, reducing fallback-fragment noise in canonical metadata.
- Canonical statement splitting is now backtick-quote-aware:
  - `_split_action_ir_statements(...)` now tracks backtick-quoted strings with escape handling,
  - semicolons inside backtick payload strings are ignored by top-level statement splitting, keeping canonical RAW_PERL fallback payloads intact.
- Canonical statement splitting is now slash-quote-aware:
  - `_split_action_ir_statements(...)` now tracks slash-delimited Perl quote-like payloads with escape handling,
  - semicolons inside slash-quote payload strings are ignored by top-level statement splitting, keeping canonical RAW_PERL fallback payloads intact.
- Canonical statement splitting is now angle-quote-aware:
  - `_split_action_ir_statements(...)` now tracks angle-delimited Perl quote-like payloads with escape and nested-angle handling,
  - semicolons inside angle-quote payload strings are ignored by top-level statement splitting, keeping canonical RAW_PERL fallback payloads intact.
- Method-value lowering now supports canonical child-call capture:
  - `call(rule)` now lowers as a method value expression, not only as a standalone compatibility wrapper,
  - `assign(scalar(retv), call(rule))` is now the preferred backend-neutral replacement for raw `$retv = call(rule)` wrapper assignments.
- Lowering documentation now treats the user-visible ActionIR surface as an explicit compatibility contract:
  - the guide set is split into a top-level hub, module-oriented guides, and `USER_GUIDE_ActionIR_EmittedPerlReference.md`,
  - the emitted-Perl reference is organized by preferred canonical helpers, compatibility helpers, and classified pass-through idioms,
  - value-expression helpers such as `scalar(...)`, `scalaref(...)`, `array(...)`, `hash(...)`, `flat(...)`, and `flatten(...)` are now documented explicitly as value-context lowerings that take effect inside consumers like `assign(...)`, `push_value(...)`, `return(payload)`, declaration initializers, and flow/control expressions.

## Change Discipline
Before each commit:
1. Update `CHANGES.md` with exact pending changes.
2. Update `DEVELOPMENT_NOTES.md` with rationale/decisions.
3. Update `USER_GUIDE.md` for user-visible behavior changes.
4. Update `ROADMAP.md` status and milestones.
5. Update `MEMORY.md` with resumable session context.
- 2026-04-07: Continued the same low-level `spec_descr(...)` contract hardening one step further. The previous slice normalized malformed outer containers and callback contracts, but malformed later entries inside an otherwise ARRAY-shaped parsed-entry list could still slip into callback-specific behavior. `Compiler.pm` now validates each parsed bootstrap entry as an `ARRAY` before invoking `compile_spec_entry(...)`, which keeps low-level descriptor assembly closer to a SOTA-style explicit boundary contract rather than “whatever the callback happens to do with malformed input.”
- 2026-04-07: Tightened the remaining low-level `spec_descr(...)` pre-entry contract seam. The previous Phase 5 slice normalized thrown `compile_spec_entry(...)` callbacks and malformed non-throwing descriptor tuples once rule iteration had already started, but two earlier contract failures still bypassed that cleaner path: invalid non-CODE callback contracts and malformed non-ARRAY parsed-entry containers. `Compiler.pm` now prepares optional runtime context first, then normalizes those pre-entry failures into the same `compiler_pipeline:spec_descr` family with top-rule-based handler identity continuity when available. This is a better fit for the diagnostics-modernization line than preserving an older raw-die contract at the low-level/public `spec_descr(...)` surface.
- Control-flow punctuation-reduction now covers attached branch bodies too: `else { ... }` and `default { ... }` are supported aliases for `else() { ... }` and `default() { ... }`. Keep unresolved-helper matching conservative so rewritten Perl `else { ... }` / `default { ... }` output is not misclassified as leftover DSL.
- 2026-03-17: Added parser-oriented `count_keys(...)` hash/object-size reducer lowering so `.spec` rules can derive one scalar key-count value from working hashes and hash-valued expressions across assignment sources, return payloads, and numeric comparison inputs, with fluent-versus-structured parity locked on both action-edge and lifecycle surfaces.
- 2026-03-17: Added parser-oriented `has_key(...)` hash/object key-presence lowering so `.spec` rules can ask whether a field exists at all across assignment sources, return payloads, and flow conditions, with fluent-versus-structured parity locked on both action-edge and lifecycle surfaces.
- 2026-03-17: Added parser-oriented array `take_last(...)` lowering so `.spec` rules can now derive one canonical trailing-suffix array value from direct arrays, projected arrays such as `sorted_keys(...)` / `sorted_values(...)`, and array-valued fallback chains too: `take_last(array_expr)` keeps the last `1` entry by default, while `take_last(array_expr, n)` keeps the last `n` entries across array assignment sources, direct return payloads, reducer composition, and nested scalar(container, index) reads, with fluent-versus-structured parity locked on both action-edge and lifecycle surfaces.
- 2026-03-17: Added explicit parser-oriented drop aliases `drop_front(...)` and `drop_back(...)` as exact lowering aliases of `tail(...)` and `drop_last(...)`, respectively, with representative fluent-versus-structured regression coverage on both action-edge and lifecycle surfaces.
- 2026-03-17: Added parser-oriented scalar boundary predicates `starts_with(...)` and `ends_with(...)` so `.spec` rules can now keep normalized prefix/suffix checks inside backend-neutral value expressions across assignments, direct return payloads, and flow conditions, with fluent-versus-structured parity locked on both action-edge and lifecycle surfaces.
- 2026-03-19: Tightened the parser-core contract before any bounded `OR{...}` / `AND{...}` expansion: the current rule-label suffixes `:&`, `:|`, `:+`, `:*`, and `:?` should now be treated as explicit supported surface, not just bootstrap-owner implementation detail.
- 2026-03-19: Tightened the split-capture contract too: `@move_pos` is preserved as a real supported parser feature and should be described as a capture-boundary cursor move, not as a standalone collector. It lowers to `$IPOS = pos $$STRING`, which is exactly why it is useful for staged coarse-to-fine parsing where stable anchors isolate harder inner substrings for a second pass.
- 2026-03-19: Naming cleanup for the split-boundary cursor is now in flight too: `@capture_from_here` is the preferred DSL spelling because it describes user intent rather than Perl mechanism, while `@move_pos` stays supported as a compatibility alias that still lowers to the same internal `MOVE_POS` event.
- 2026-03-19: First grouped-rule extension is now real: bounded repeated-choice labels `OR{N,M}`, `OR{N}`, `OR{N,}`, and `OR{,M}` now lower through explicit `rep_min` / `rep_max` metadata into the existing repeat-loop handler family. This keeps the scope intentionally narrow: bounded `AND` still needs a separate sequence-repetition design instead of being inferred from the repeated-choice implementation.
- 2026-03-19: The next grouped-rule slice is now real too: bounded repeated-sequence labels `AND{N,M}`, `AND{N}`, `AND{N,}`, and `AND{,M}` now lower through explicit `rep_min` / `rep_max` metadata into dedicated repeated-sequence handler variants. Metadata is now locked on both action and blind-call sequence paths, with representative runtime checks on bounded ordered repetition too, so repeated ordered sequences are explicit supported surface instead of being hand-waved as future work or mis-modeled as repeated choice.
- 2026-03-19: Plain standalone `OR` is now real too, but the contract stays precise. `rule:OR` now lowers as the explicit worded repeated-choice label with the same repetition-family metadata contract as `rule:OR{1,}`. Bare historical `rule:` remains the author-facing baseline mental model for that family, not a promise that every low-level emitted handler path is textually identical. Plain standalone `AND` remains deferred and is regression-locked as rejected syntax for now.
- 2026-03-19: Captured the higher-level `.spec` file-format explanation in the user docs too: `.spec` files should be taught as paragraph-oriented rule files, not as a mysterious line-oriented language. The useful structural rule is simple: a rule starts at `name:` / `name::`, ends at the next rule start or EOF, and everything in between is part of that rule paragraph. That explanation should make the frontend shape easier for users to understand without overfitting the docs to helper-method details.
- 2026-03-19: Captured another important general-format rule in the user docs: action-edge indexing is regex-slot based. `-> rule` is the same author-facing surface as `-> rule[0]`, and `-> rule[N]` means “target the `(N+1)`th regex of that rule.” This belongs in the general `.spec` structure explanation because it is core rule-body semantics, not helper-method detail.
- 2026-03-19: Tightened the same note with the practical usage rule too: while regex-slot indexing is mechanically general, `rule[N]` is mainly a same-rule recursive entry selector in real specs. Cross-rule calls are now documented as normally using the first entrypoint (`-> B` / `-> B[0]`) instead of higher regex-slot indices.
- 2026-03-19: Plain standalone `AND` is now real too. `rule:AND` now lowers as the explicit worded ordered-sequence label with the same sequence-family runtime behavior as `rule:&`, while keeping a distinct metadata node so the supported spelling is regression-lockable and documentable. That lets the guide teach `AND` as the worded sibling of `:&` while leaving only `AND+` deferred in this family.
- 2026-03-19: Plain standalone `AND+` is now real too. `rule:AND+` now lowers as the explicit open-ended repeated-sequence shorthand with the same repetition-family metadata contract and runtime behavior as `rule:AND{1,}`, while keeping a distinct metadata node so the shorthand spelling is regression-lockable and easy to document in the guide.
- 2026-03-19: Narrowed one concrete parser-core hardening seam too: `LinkedSpec::Validation` now parses the current rule-label surface instead of only recognizing `name::` as a real rule definition during DSL checks. It now accepts explicit `AND+`, bounded grouped labels, and inline same-line regex/capture rule headers; it also rejects duplicate regular-rule labels across current mode spellings and catches malformed grouped suffixes like `AND{,}` earlier.
- 2026-03-20: Started taking a concrete Phase 5 runtime/diagnostics slice instead of only frontend work. The generated default action-edge handler in `SpecEntry.pm` no longer prints a handler-generation banner and hard `exit 1` when the `LinkedRE::or(...)` dispatch seam dies; it now throws the same failure context as a normal eval-visible runtime error so callers can trap/report it without process termination. This is still not the full runtime-modernization end state, but it removes one explicit abrupt-exit seam from the hot parse path.
- 2026-03-20: Continued the same Phase 5 line by giving the compiler pipeline a structured failure breadcrumb instead of only `undef` plus trace output. `LinkedSpec::Compiler::run_get_pipeline(...)` now populates `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` with a small stable hash (`type`, `stage`, `summary`, `detail`) when validation, bootstrap parse, spec-descriptor build, or generated-descriptor validation fails, and it clears stale `last_error` state on successful entry. This is still injected-owner/internal surface rather than end-user API, but it creates a consistent place for callers and future diagnostics work to inspect the last pipeline failure without scraping trace text.
- 2026-03-20: Continued the same Phase 5 line one layer higher by making `LinkedSpec::Runtime::run_get(...)` expose its live per-run runtime context through opt-in `runtime_ctx_ref => \$ctx`. That keeps the return shape stable while letting `Runtime::run_get(...)` and the public `LinkedSpec::Get(...)` façade surface the same `top_rule`, parser-source chunk capture, and structured `last_error` payload that the compiler pipeline now maintains internally. This is a good middle ground for diagnostics modernization: more reachable structured state without prematurely redesigning the main public return contract.
- 2026-03-20: Continued that same Phase 5 line into `LinkedSpec::ParserFactory`. `get_parser(...)` now keeps a shared diagnostics context even when the failure happens before `Runtime::run_get(...)` begins. `ParserFactory::run_get_parser(...)` initializes/uses `runtime_ctx_ref => \$ctx`, records parser-factory structured failures for spec-name validation, spec-path resolution, and spec-file loading, and seeds `spec_name` / resolved `spec_path` there. `Runtime::run_get(...)` now reuses an existing `runtime_ctx_ref` hash instead of replacing it, so file-oriented callers keep one continuous context across parser-factory and compiler/runtime phases.
- 2026-03-20: Tightened the structured diagnostics payload itself. `last_error` now carries `owner_stage` plus `spec_name` / `spec_path` when known on both parser-factory and compiler-pipeline failures. That keeps the payload closer to a portable failure record instead of forcing callers to merge separate context fields after the fact. The immediate payoff is on the `get_parser(...)` path, where a persisted `last_error` record now already contains the requested spec name, resolved spec path when available, and a stable combined owner-stage identifier like `parser_factory:resolve_spec_path` or `compiler_pipeline:validate_spec_content`.
- 2026-03-20: Continued the same Phase 5 thread into actual parser execution. `SpecEntry.pm` now records structured `runtime_handler` failures in `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` when a compiled handler eval fails, including `rule_label`, `handler_variant`, and preserved spec identity when the parser came from `get_parser(...)`. `Compiler.pm` now clears stale `last_error` state at top-level parser invocation entry, so the runtime diagnostics channel behaves like a per-invocation failure record instead of a sticky one-shot latch.
- 2026-03-20: Continued the same Phase 5 runtime/diagnostics line one layer higher. `Compiler.pm` now also promotes outer top-rule invocation dies into structured `runtime_parser` payloads in `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` before rethrowing them. The important nuance is that the wrapper restores inner `runtime_handler` `$@` behavior when the handler returned normally but recorded an eval-visible failure, so existing runtime-eval semantics and diagnostics stay intact while the previously unstructured outer-die seam becomes inspectable too.
- 2026-03-21: Continued the same Phase 5 line back into compile-time descriptor assembly. `Compiler.pm` now traps exceptions thrown while `spec_descr(...)` is compiling parsed rule entries and while `_build_final_descriptor(...)` is assembling the final descriptor / `gdata`, then normalizes those failures into the same `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` `compiler_pipeline` payload family (`spec_descr` / `build_final_descriptor`) instead of letting them escape as raw outer dies. This is a good fit for the diagnostics-continuity goal because callers using `runtime_ctx_ref => \$ctx` now get the same stable failure channel even when the compiler dies inside injected or lower-level descriptor-build callbacks.
- 2026-03-21: Continued that same Phase 5 diagnostics normalization across the earlier compiler stages too. `Compiler.pm` now traps exceptions from `validate_spec_content(...)`, `validate_dsl_syntax(...)`, `bootstrap_parse(...)`, and `validate_gdata_references(...)` and records them in the same structured `compiler_pipeline` `last_error` channel instead of letting those stages punch through as raw callback dies. That closes an important consistency gap: callers using `runtime_ctx_ref => \$ctx` now get the same diagnostics contract whether a compiler stage fails by returning false or by throwing.
- 2026-03-21: Continued the same Phase 5 diagnostics normalization into `ParserFactory.pm`. The file-oriented `get_parser(...)` path now traps exceptions from `validate_spec_name(...)`, `resolve_spec_path(...)`, `load_spec_content(...)`, and the delegated `compile_spec(...)` callback and records parser-factory `last_error` payloads for those stages instead of leaking raw callback dies. The subtle but important behavior is on `compile_spec(...)`: if the deeper runtime/compiler owner already wrote a structured `last_error` record before throwing, `ParserFactory` now preserves that richer payload instead of clobbering it with a generic wrapper-stage error.
- 2026-03-21: Continued the same Phase 5 diagnostics normalization one layer down into `Runtime.pm`. `LinkedSpec::Runtime::run_get(...)` now traps raw dies coming back from `Compiler::run_get_pipeline(...)` and records a fallback `runtime_owner` payload (`runtime_owner:run_get_pipeline`) only when no deeper structured `last_error` record exists yet. That keeps the runtime owner from hiding richer compiler/runtime diagnostics while still ensuring `Runtime::run_get(...)` and `LinkedSpec::Get(...)` expose a stable structured failure when the compiler delegation seam itself dies before deeper normalization happens.
- 2026-03-21: Continued the same Phase 5 diagnostics normalization back into `Compiler.pm` itself. `LinkedSpec::Compiler::run_get_pipeline(...)` now treats callback/runtime-owner preparation as an explicit owned stage too: failures while resolving/bootstraping `bootstrap_parse`, `compile_spec_entry`, or validation-owner setup now normalize into `compiler_pipeline:prepare_pipeline` when a runtime context already exists. That narrows the remaining role of the `runtime_owner:run_get_pipeline` fallback to cases where the compiler owner could not normalize anything more specific first.
- 2026-03-21: Continued the same Phase 5 diagnostics normalization into `ParserFactory.pm` setup too. `LinkedSpec::ParserFactory::run_get_parser(...)` now treats callback/trace/dependency preparation as an explicit owned stage: failures while building default deps, resolving required callbacks/values, or entering the initial trace scope now normalize into `parser_factory:prepare_parser_factory` when the file-oriented owner has a runtime context hook available. That keeps the parser-factory owner on the same model as the compiler owner: normalize setup-stage failures locally instead of leaving them to broader wrapper fallbacks.
- 2026-03-21: Added grouped action-edge target support for the shared structured code-block form. The new surface is intentionally narrow and explicit: `-> RuleA | RuleB { ... }` is supported and expands during bootstrap parse into ordinary duplicated `ACODE` entries with the same code payload, so RuleIR/runtime semantics do not need a separate grouped-edge execution model. Validation now treats grouped targets as real action edges and rejects grouped forms that omit the shared `{ ... }` block.
- 2026-03-21: Continued the same Phase 5 diagnostics line into the top-rule contract seam of the returned parser closure in `Compiler.pm`. The closure now checks that a selected top rule label exists, that the compiled descriptor has an entry for that rule, and that the entry exposes a handler coderef before attempting invocation. If any of those contracts are broken, the closure now records a structured `runtime_parser` payload at `resolve_top_rule_handler` and then dies with a targeted message, instead of falling through to a lower-level undefined-subroutine style failure.
- 2026-03-21: Extended the same Phase 5 diagnostics hook ergonomics on both owner paths. `Runtime.pm` and `ParserFactory.pm` now accept direct shared hashrefs for `runtime_ctx_ref` in addition to the earlier scalar-slot forms, so callers can use a natural `runtime_ctx_ref => \%ctx` / `$ctx_hashref` shape and keep seed fields in that same shared hash while LinkedSpec adds `top_rule`, `spec_name`, and `last_error` data in place.
- 2026-03-21: Re-aligned the public `LinkedSpec::Get(...)` façade with its own documented option contract. The current façade code had drifted back to raw `%option = @args`, even though earlier Phase 1A work and the notes already treated `Get(...)` as a normalized flat key/value wrapper like `get_parser(...)`. `Get(...)` now uses the same even-pair normalization and odd-trailing fallback-to-empty-hash behavior, and regression coverage traps `Runtime::run_get(...)` directly so that contract cannot silently drift again.
- 2026-03-21: Continued the same Phase 1A façade cleanup as a no-behavior-change structural refactor. `LinkedSpec.pm` now centralizes its lazy owner dispatch through one `_dispatch_owner_call(...)` helper instead of repeating `_require_pkg(...) unless ...->can(...)` plus direct package-call boilerplate across every public wrapper. This keeps the façade thinner and easier to audit without changing the public APIs, and the existing regression suite already covers those wrapper surfaces end to end.
- 2026-03-21: Continued the same Phase 5 runtime-modernization line in `SpecEntry.pm`. `_build_runtime_handler(...)` no longer string-evals the full generated handler source on every invocation. It now compiles that generated source once into a cached coderef on the first call and reuses that coderef for later invocations, which reduces one hot-path eval seam without changing the external rule contract. Invalid generated handler source now records an explicit structured `runtime_handler:rule_handler_compile` failure instead of being folded into the generic runtime-handler eval stage, and regression coverage now locks both compile-once reuse and the new compile-failure stage.
- 2026-03-21: Continued that same Phase 5 line into the repeated-handler builders too. The generated `REP_BCODE`, `REP_AND_BCODE`, and `REP_AND_ACODE` variants no longer synthesize nested helper closures as `sub { eval '...' }`. Because the outer handler source is already compiled and cached now, those repeat helpers can be emitted as ordinary nested anonymous subs directly in the generated source. That removes another inner runtime eval seam while keeping the repeat-rule contract unchanged, and regression coverage now locks both the selected repeat variants and the absence of the old eval-wrapped nested helper subs in captured parser source.
- 2026-03-21: Continued that same Phase 5 line into the default `LinkedRE` dispatch seam inside generated handlers. The main handler loop no longer uses quoted-string `eval q/$minfo = LinkedRE::or(...)/`; it now uses a normal block `eval { LinkedRE::or(...) }` wrapper. That keeps the existing failure banner and error-detail surface intact while removing one more runtime string-eval seam from emitted handler source. Regression coverage now locks the new parser-source shape too.
- 2026-03-22: Continued that same Phase 5 line one step further at the same generated-handler dispatch seam. The default handler loop now calls `LinkedRE::or(...)` directly with no inner eval wrapper at all, and the existing outer `runtime_handler` trap becomes the only owner of failures coming out of that dispatch path. This is a better fit for the modernization goal than keeping a second per-iteration eval/banner layer in the hot path, and regression coverage now locks both the direct parser-source shape and the dropped inner-banner expectation.
- 2026-03-22: Continued the same Phase 5 `RuntimeContext` extraction one level deeper into the error-builder contract. The owner modules had already centralized structured `last_error` creation, but they were still each hand-applying their own default type (`runtime_owner`, `parser_factory`, `compiler_pipeline`, `runtime_handler`) before calling the shared helper. That defaulting rule now lives in `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` too, which keeps one more part of the structured diagnostics contract defined in a single place instead of being repeated across owner modules.
- 2026-03-22: Continued that same owner-side cleanup in `SpecEntry.pm`. The module still had one inline direct call into `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` for top-rule state publication after rule compilation. That write now routes through a tiny local owner helper instead. It does not change behavior, but it keeps the remaining shared-runtime-context write surface easier to audit and makes the owner boundary slightly more uniform.
- 2026-03-22: Continued the Phase 1A façade-thinning line with a very small but worthwhile cleanup in `LinkedSpec.pm`. `Get(...)` and `get_parser(...)` were still each carrying their own copy of the same flat option-pair normalization logic. That normalization now lives in one tiny local helper so the remaining façade wrapper code stays more uniform and the public option contract is defined in one place instead of two.
- 2026-03-22: Continued the same Phase 5 owner-cleanup line in `ParserFactory.pm`. The module still had a small cluster of wrappers that all repeated the same pattern: ensure `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` is loaded, then directly call one shared-owner subroutine. That routing now goes through one tiny local owner helper instead. No behavior changes, but it keeps one more owner boundary a bit smaller and easier to audit.
- 2026-03-21: Continued that same Phase 5 line into cached handler construction timing. `_build_runtime_handler(...)` now compiles generated handler source eagerly when the runtime wrapper is built, rather than on first invocation. That removes another runtime hot-path string-eval seam while preserving the current external contract: successful handlers still compile once and reuse the cached coderef, and invalid generated source still does not populate `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` until the wrapper is actually invoked.
- 2026-03-21: Tightened the eager-handler-compile diagnostics seam too. Invalid generated handler source can emit Perl compile warnings before failing; `SpecEntry.pm` now captures those warnings during eager wrapper construction and folds them into the later `runtime_handler:rule_handler_compile` detail instead of leaking them to stderr. That keeps the eager-compile change quiet in normal regression/CI output while preserving the diagnostic content in structured `last_error`.
- 2026-03-21: Continued the same Phase 1A façade cleanup with one more monolith-era residue removal. `LinkedSpec.pm` no longer enables `use re 'eval'` at compile time; the current façade is only a thin owner-dispatch layer and does not use regex code-eval constructs itself, so keeping that pragma there was unnecessary compile-surface baggage.
- 2026-03-21: Tightened the parser-return side of the Phase 5 runtime diagnostics contract. `Compiler.pm` used to preserve a stale `runtime_handler` payload in `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` even if the top-level parser invocation ultimately succeeded and returned a defined AST. The returned parser now clears that stale inner-handler state on success, while still preserving the old `$@` / `last_error` behavior when a top-level handler really fails and returns `undef`.
- 2026-03-21: Continued the same Phase 5 diagnostics cleanup at the owner-structure level. `Runtime.pm`, `ParserFactory.pm`, `Compiler.pm`, and `SpecEntry.pm` all carried their own copies of the structured `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` payload-builder logic. That helper now lives in a shared `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` module so the owner-stage/spec-name/spec-path/rule-label/handler-variant contract is defined in one place instead of four.
- 2026-03-21: Continued the same `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` extraction one step further. The SCALAR/HASH/shared-hash-slot `runtime_ctx_ref` coercion and seeded-context materialization logic that used to live separately in `Runtime.pm` and `ParserFactory.pm` now also lives in the shared helper, which removes another duplicated runtime-context contract seam and keeps the option-validation wording in one place.
- 2026-03-21: Continued that same shared-owner extraction into parser-source handling too. The small helpers that used to allocate parser-source chunk arrays, wire the emit callback, and append parser-source chunks from Runtime/Compiler/SpecEntry are now owned by `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` instead, which keeps more of the runtime context contract in one place and shrinks the surrounding owner modules.
- 2026-03-21: Continued that same shared-owner extraction into the remaining direct state-field writes. `top_rule` reset/update and resolved `spec_path` assignment now also go through `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext`, which removes another small batch of handwritten runtime-context mutations from Runtime/ParserFactory/SpecEntry and keeps more of the state contract centralized.
- 2026-03-22: Continued that same shared-owner extraction into fallback structured-error preservation too. `Runtime.pm` and `ParserFactory.pm` were still each hand-checking whether `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` already held a structured payload before writing their own fallback owner errors. That “set fallback only when no deeper structured payload exists yet” rule now also lives in `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext`, which keeps more of the diagnostics contract in one place.
- 2026-03-22: Continued that same shared-owner extraction into runtime-context reads too. `Compiler.pm` still reached directly into `runtime_ctx->{top_rule}` and `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` for parser-ready tracing and recovered-runtime-handler cleanup. `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` now owns helper readers for those values, including structured-error type/detail accessors, so more of the read-side diagnostics/state contract now lives in one place too.
- 2026-03-22: Continued that same shared-owner extraction into parser-source reads too. `Compiler.pm` still read `runtime_ctx->{parser_source_chunks_ref}` directly during pipeline setup after the earlier parser-source capture extraction. `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` now owns a read-side accessor for that slot as well, which keeps more of the parser-source state contract in the shared owner instead of leaving one direct read behind in Compiler.
- 2026-03-22: Continued that same shared-owner extraction into `Runtime::run_get(...)` preparation too. The `Runtime.pm` owner was still manually clearing stale `top_rule` state and wiring parser-source capture for each invocation after normalizing the injected context hook. That per-run preparation step now also lives in `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext`, which keeps more of the runtime-context lifecycle contract out of the owner module.
- 2026-03-22: Continued that same shared-owner extraction into `ParserFactory::run_get_parser(...)` preparation too. The parser-factory owner was still manually normalizing `runtime_ctx_ref` and seeding `spec_name` into the shared context before entering resolution/load/compile flow. That file-oriented preparation step now also lives in `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext`, which keeps more of the runtime-context lifecycle contract out of the owner module there as well.
- 2026-03-22: Continued the same Phase 5 owner cleanup with a no-behavior-change `RuntimeContext` lazy-load refactor. Runtime, ParserFactory, Compiler, and SpecEntry all still repeated the same `unless LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext->can(...)` guard at each individual call site. Each owner now routes that through one tiny local helper instead, which makes the remaining delegation surface easier to audit without changing the lazy-load contract.
- 2026-03-22: `SpecEntry::compile_spec_entry(...)` no longer checks a standalone `emit_parser_source_line` dep hook. Parser-source emission now goes only through injected `runtime_ctx`, which matches the current Phase 5 owner story and removes one leftover split-responsibility seam from spec-entry compilation.
- 2026-03-22: `Compiler.pm` no longer hand-prepares `parser_source_chunks_ref` on injected runtime contexts. That small setup step now lives in `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` as part of the same Phase 5 owner-cleanup line, which keeps compiler-owned runtime-context preparation aligned with the earlier `run_get(...)`, `get_parser(...)`, and spec-entry cleanup work.
- 2026-03-22: `Runtime.pm` no longer hand-normalizes `runtime_ctx_ref` and `dump_parser_source` before preparing per-run runtime state. That option-level `run_get(...)` setup now lives in `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext`, which keeps the runtime owner aligned with the earlier parser-factory and compiler-side preparation cleanup work.
- 2026-03-24: Continued the plugin/runtime modernization track by spending public plugin lookup inside smaller repo-owned `.plg` files too. `qc_summary.plg` and `skew.plg` now resolve known plugin names through `LinkedSpec::get_plugin(...)` instead of `PPlugin->get(...)`. Future resume should keep treating direct repo-owned `PPlugin->get(...)` use as a migration target when the plugin name is already explicit and stable.
- 2026-03-24: Continued the plugin/runtime modernization track inside lightweight `.plg` files too. `regtest.plg` and `string.plg` no longer call `PPlugin` directly for known plugin names; they now use public `LinkedSpec::run_plugin(...)` instead. Future resume should keep treating direct repo-owned `.plg` use of `new PPlugin`, `PPlugin->exec(...)`, and `PPlugin->exec_plugin_name(...)` as migration targets when the plugin name is already known.
- 2026-03-24: Continued the plugin/runtime modernization track by trimming stale inheritance, not just direct callsites. `RTLUtils.pm` and `LispML.pm` no longer advertise `PPlugin` in `@ISA`, because current repo-owned usage no longer depends on inherited plugin behavior there. Future resume should treat stray repo-owned `PPlugin` inheritance as a migration smell unless a class is still intentionally acting as a legacy plugin-runtime adapter.
- 2026-04-08: Continued the same compiler-state extraction line into migration-summary shaping too. `LinkedSpec::CompilerState` now owns the `action_rewriter_migration` summary reduction over compiled-spec state, while `Compiler.pm` keeps only the optional trace dump around that owner-produced result. The owner-routing regression now treats migration-summary shaping as part of the same extracted boundary.
- 2026-04-08: Continued the same compiler-state extraction line into descriptor metadata assembly too. `LinkedSpec::CompilerState` now owns the helper that turns compiled-spec state plus compiler-supplied additions like `parse_mode` and migration summary into final descriptor metadata, so `Compiler.pm` no longer mutates owner metadata locally at that seam. The owner-routing regression now treats descriptor-metadata assembly as part of the same extracted boundary.
- 2026-04-08: Continued the same compiler-state extraction line into by-label compiled-rule lookup too. `LinkedSpec::CompilerState` now owns the preferred dependency-existence and dependency-rule-info lookups used by `spec_gdata(...)`, so `Compiler.pm` no longer probes the compiled rule map directly for that seam. The owner-routing regression now treats by-label lookup as part of the same extracted boundary.
- 2026-04-08: Continued the same compiler-state extraction line into ordered rule iteration too. `LinkedSpec::CompilerState` now owns one canonical ordered compiled-rule view, so `Compiler.pm` no longer rebuilds the active rule stream locally from `rule_order + rules_by_label` for migration-summary work or `spec_gdata(...)` iteration. The owner-routing regression now treats that ordered iteration seam as part of the same extracted boundary.
- 2026-04-08: Continued the same compiler-state extraction line into read-side state access too. `LinkedSpec::CompilerState` now owns the preferred accessor path for compiled-spec redefined-rule labels and descriptor-owned rule maps, so `Compiler.pm` trace/logging and `Validation.pm` descriptor validation no longer reach into raw compiled-state fields directly. The owner-routing regression now treats those read-side calls as part of the same extracted seam.
- 2026-04-08: Continued the same compiler-state extraction line into the last remaining compatibility-shape normalization seams. `LinkedSpec::CompilerState` now also owns the “accept legacy compiled-spec hash or compiled-spec state” and “accept legacy compiled-gdata hash or compiled-gdata state” conversions, so `Compiler.pm` no longer keeps a second local normalization copy beside the extracted state owner. Focused regression coverage now traps both normalization routes as part of the same owner-routing contract.
- 2026-03-22: `Compiler.pm` no longer hand-assembles final parser-source text from `parser_source_chunks_ref`. That final flush/output step now lives in `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext`, which makes the parser-source lifecycle consistent end to end: allocation, capture, read-side access, and final delivery all now live under the same shared owner.
- 2026-03-22: Continued that same shared-owner cleanup into reused-context spec identity too. A shared runtime context reused across `get_parser(...)` and inline `Get(...)` calls could still leak stale `spec_name` / `spec_path` into later structured failures. `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` now clears stale file identity on inline `run_get(...)` preparation by default, while letting the parser-factory path explicitly preserve file identity across delegated compilation, and `get_parser(...)` preparation now also clears stale `spec_path` / `top_rule` before starting a new resolution flow.
- 2026-03-22: Continued that same shared-owner cleanup into reused parser-source capture too. A shared runtime context reused across multiple compiles could still append stale parser-source chunks from an earlier run because the shared `parser_source_chunks_ref` arrayref was preserved without clearing its contents. `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` now clears that shared chunk buffer at the start of each compile while keeping the same arrayref alive, so reused parser-source capture reflects the current compile only.
- 2026-03-22: Continued that same shared-owner cleanup into parser-factory-side parser-source hygiene too. A shared runtime context reused across compiles and later `get_parser(...)` failures could still leave old parser-source chunks in place because parser-factory preparation refreshed spec identity without touching parser-source state. `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` now clears stale parser-source chunks and removes the stale emit callback during `get_parser(...)` preparation as well, which keeps early file-oriented failures from inheriting parser-source output from a previous compile.
- 2026-03-22: Continued that same shared-owner cleanup into `Runtime.pm` itself. The runtime owner still had a small cluster of wrappers repeating the same pattern: ensure `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` is loaded, then call one shared-owner subroutine. That routing now goes through one tiny local owner helper instead, which keeps the runtime-side delegation surface more uniform and easier to audit without changing behavior.
- 2026-03-22: Continued that same shared-owner cleanup into `Compiler.pm` too. The compiler owner still had the largest remaining cluster of tiny wrappers repeating the same pattern: ensure `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` is loaded, then call one shared-owner subroutine for runtime-context preparation, parser-source, or structured-error state. That routing now goes through one tiny local owner helper instead, which keeps the compiler-side delegation surface smaller and more consistent with Runtime and ParserFactory.
- 2026-03-22: Continued that same shared-owner cleanup into `SpecEntry.pm` as well. The spec-entry owner still had one small cluster of wrappers repeating the same pattern: ensure `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` is loaded, then call one shared-owner subroutine for runtime-handler error capture, parser-source emission, or discovered `top_rule` state. That routing now goes through one tiny local owner helper instead, which keeps the spec-entry side aligned with the same owner-dispatch cleanup already landed in Runtime, ParserFactory, and Compiler.
- 2026-03-22: Continued the same Phase 5 tracing/instrumentation line in `Compiler.pm`. The returned parser closure now owns an explicit top-level `LinkedSpec::parser_invoke:<top_rule>` trace scope with `resolve_top_rule_handler` and `invoke_top_rule:<top_rule>` decisions, which gives runtime tracing one clean bridge between compile-time scopes and the nested `rule_handler` scopes emitted by actual rule execution.
- 2026-03-22: Continued the same Phase 5 diagnostics-improvement line in `SpecEntry.pm`. Generated-handler compile failures are already structured as `runtime_handler:rule_handler_compile`, but they still looked like anonymous eval text. The handler wrapper now compiles generated source under a stable synthetic label (`LinkedSpec::generated_handler:<rule_label>:<handler_variant>`), so the preserved compile-detail text is much easier to attribute during debugging and log review.
- 2026-03-22: Continued that same Phase 5 diagnostics-improvement line one step further. The generated-handler source label is no longer available only inside Perl compile/eval text; `RuntimeContext` now also preserves it as explicit structured `handler_source_label` data on `runtime_handler` failures, so callers can attribute failures directly from `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` without scraping `detail`.
- 2026-03-22: Continued that same Phase 5 diagnostics-improvement line across the outer parser boundary too. `runtime_parser` failures already carried `rule_label` plus `handler_variant` when the top-level parser still knew the selected compiled rule handler. They now also carry the same structured `handler_source_label`, built by a shared `RuntimeContext` helper, so outer parser failures and inner rule-handler failures expose one consistent generated-handler identity field.
- 2026-03-22: Started the preferred Phase 3 line with an option-level parse-mode slice rather than a DSL syntax expansion. The public surface now accepts `parse_mode => 'seek' | 'consume'` through `Get(...)` / `get_parser(...)`, default behavior remains backward-compatible `seek`, generated descriptors now record `meta->{parse_mode}`, and `SpecEntry.pm` only emits an explicit third `LinkedRE::or(...)` argument when contiguous `consume` semantics are requested. Invalid parse modes are normalized through the existing compiler-owner setup failure channel at `compiler_pipeline:prepare_pipeline`, which keeps the new option aligned with the current structured diagnostics contract.
- 2026-03-22: Captured an important Phase 3 design clarification in the docs before more semantics land: `seek` / `consume` should be treated as cursor discipline, while `OR` / `AND` stays the rule-composition axis. The fact that `seek` feels extraction-like and `consume` feels grammar-like is useful intuition, but future implementation should not collapse those concepts together.
- 2026-03-22: Captured another important Phase 3 semantic boundary before deeper execution work continues: the current LinkedSpec / `.spec` model is not a clean fit for full parser-engine backtracking. The runtime is fundamentally forward-moving and action-bearing, and the existing `BACKTRACK()` / `IBACKTRACK()` helpers are local cursor rewinds, not a commitment to global state rollback across choice trees, captures, AST assembly, and side effects.
- 2026-03-22: Started Phase 4 with one intentionally small first-class checkpoint slice instead of trying to design the whole capture API up front. `.spec` now has named `@mark(name)` checkpoints in addition to anonymous `@capture_from_here`, and the compatibility helper surface now has `capture_from(name)` for “text since that named mark.”
- 2026-03-23: Hardened that first named-checkpoint slice after reviewing the scoping and timing risks. `@mark(name)` is no longer a shared parse-path surface; it now lowers under the current rule label and only fires when the regex slot that carries the mark actually matches, which makes same-name reuse across different rules safe and keeps child rules from accidentally reading a parent rule's mark.
- 2026-03-23: Expanded the named-checkpoint documentation immediately after that hardening pass. The main user guide now teaches the exact current semantics that matter in practice: `capture_from(name)` captures to the left edge of the current match, `@mark(name)` is a later-slot surface rather than a same-slot action helper, the feature is rule-local rather than child-shared, and the guide now has multiple worked examples so it reads like usable authoring guidance rather than a bare syntax note.
- 2026-03-23: Tightened the documentation style expectation too: normal user-guide examples should prefer backend-neutral helper syntax in code blocks, with Perl-specific lowering shown in the emitted-reference guides instead of being the default teaching surface.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next small Phase 4 helper rather than overloading `capture_from(name)` with surprising mutation. `capture_from(name)` stays the stable read from a rule-local mark, while new `capture_take(name)` returns the same span and then advances that rule-local mark to the current parser position so repeated delimiter-style capture can behave like a named split cursor explicitly.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next complementary Phase 4 helper after that split. `mark_here(name)` now lets a later action block set or move the same rule-local named mark explicitly without first reading through it, which makes the API triangle clearer: stable read (`capture_from`), read+advance (`capture_take`), explicit write (`mark_here`).
- 2026-03-23: Added the next complementary Phase 4 helper after that too. `clear_mark(name)` now lets a later action block delete the same rule-local named mark explicitly, so the capture/mark surface is no longer “write-only except overwrite”: rules can now read stably, read+advance, write explicitly, and clear explicitly.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next complementary Phase 4 helper after `clear_mark(name)` too. `mark_exists(name)` now lets a later action block ask whether that same rule-local named mark is currently present, without reading or mutating the checkpoint, so the surface now also has an explicit presence probe instead of forcing that check through a read helper.
- 2026-03-23: Followed that immediately with a contract lock rather than leaving it as accidental behavior. `mark_exists(name)` now has explicit regression and documentation coverage for backend-neutral flow branching too, so `if(mark_exists(name)); ...; else; ...; endif` is part of the supported named-mark surface rather than a lucky side effect of helper substitution order.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next Phase 4 helper after that branchability lock. `capture_between(start_mark, end_mark)` now lets a rule read a span between two explicit rule-local named checkpoints instead of always using the current match edge as the right boundary, which makes the mark surface useful for remembered intermediate boundaries and side-by-side “two-mark span versus current-edge span” patterns.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next companion helper after that two-mark read. `mark_match_start(name)` now stores the left edge of the current match instead of post-match `pos $$STRING`, which resolves the practical “end_rule should point before its own token text” gap and makes the distinction between post-match checkpoints and current-match-left-edge checkpoints explicit in the language.
- 2026-03-23: Continued the same Phase 4 mark/checkpoint work into runtime observability. The checkpoint API is only really usable if authors can see where a stored mark actually landed, so high/debug trace output now shows mark-write positions for `@mark(name)`, `mark_here(name)`, `mark_match_start(name)`, and the advancing write inside `capture_take(name)` as a small visible input excerpt plus a caret under the stored checkpoint position.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next complementary Phase 4 helper after that trace-visibility pass. `capture_take_between(start_mark, end_mark)` now keeps the clarity of explicit remembered boundaries while still letting the start mark roll forward after the read, so the API now has a stable explicit two-mark read (`capture_between`) and an advancing explicit two-mark read (`capture_take_between`) instead of forcing authors to hand-compose that advance step.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next complementary Phase 4 helper after that. `mark_pos(name)` now exposes the stored numeric checkpoint value itself without mutating the rule-local mark bucket, so the current API now has a cleaner split between presence checks (`mark_exists`), position reads (`mark_pos`), span reads (`capture_from` / `capture_between`), and advancing reads (`capture_take` / `capture_take_between`).
- 2026-03-23: Added the next complementary Phase 4 helper after that too. `capture_len_from(name)` now returns the numeric width of the same current-edge span that `capture_from(name)` would read, so the mark/checkpoint API now distinguishes cleanly between substring reads (`capture_from`), numeric current-edge width reads (`capture_len_from`), position reads (`mark_pos`), and advancing reads (`capture_take` / `capture_take_between`) without forcing rules to materialize text just to learn its length.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next complementary Phase 4 helper after that too. `capture_len_between(start_mark, end_mark)` now returns the numeric width of the same explicit remembered-boundary span that `capture_between(start_mark, end_mark)` would read, so the mark/checkpoint API now distinguishes cleanly between one-mark current-edge width reads and two-mark explicit-boundary width reads without forcing rules to materialize text just to learn its length.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next complementary Phase 4 helper after that too. `mark_copy(target_mark, source_mark)` now lets a rule copy one remembered explicit boundary into another named checkpoint, and missing-source copies now clear the target instead of leaving stale state behind. That keeps pure numeric read helpers pure while still giving the language an explicit boundary-move primitive for later same-rule orchestration.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next complementary Phase 4 helpers after that too. `match_start_pos()` and `match_end_pos()` now read the current local match boundaries directly, without round-tripping through the named-mark bucket at all. That makes the capture/mark API cleaner in an important way: stored-checkpoint reads (`mark_pos`) and current-match boundary reads are now separate first-class concepts instead of being encoded through raw Perl or through temporary mark writes.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next complementary Phase 4 helper after that too. `match_text()` now reads the current local match text directly, which completes the small “current local match” family: text, left edge, and right edge are now all available as backend-neutral reads without storing a checkpoint first or teaching raw `$LMATCH` as the default user-facing surface.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next complementary Phase 4 helper after that too. `match_len()` now reads the width of the current local match directly, which makes the small local-match family fully uniform: text, width, left edge, and right edge can now all be taught without dropping back to raw `$LMATCH` arithmetic in normal user-facing examples.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next complementary Phase 4 helper after that too. `entry_text()` now reads the current immediate match text directly, which makes the user-facing match-read surface more symmetric and gives the guides a clean explicit way to teach the difference between entry/immediate match text and active local match text. While landing that helper, two exposed runtime gaps showed up in the new multi-rule example too: parser build still let the last compiled rule win as the effective top-level entry, and repeated `Get(...)` calls on the same inline spec scalar ref could silently see an empty spec because validation/bootstrap parse left the regex cursor at end-of-text. `Compiler.pm` now resolves the selected top rule after compilation by defaulting to the first parsed rule paragraph and honoring explicit `top_rule => 'RuleName'` overrides before parser emission and invocation, and it now resets the spec-string cursor before DSL validation and bootstrap parse so repeated inline compiles stay stable.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next complementary Phase 4 helpers after that too. `entry_group(index)` and `match_group(index)` now read capture groups from the immediate-entry side and the current-local side directly, which starts shrinking the last obviously Perl-flavored `IMATCH_LIST` / `LMATCH_LIST` access patterns out of normal user-facing examples without forcing list-context helpers yet.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next complementary Phase 4 helper after that too. `entry_len()` now reads the width of the current immediate match directly, which makes the small immediate-match family more uniform: text, width, left edge, and right edge can now all be taught without dropping back to raw `$IMATCH` arithmetic in normal user-facing examples.
- 2026-03-23: Added the next complementary Phase 4 helpers after that too. `entry_start_pos()` and `entry_end_pos()` now expose the immediate entry-match left and right boundaries directly, which finishes the small “current immediate match” family: text, left edge, and right edge now all have explicit backend-neutral helper forms without falling back to raw `$IMATCH` / `$IPOS` arithmetic.
- 2026-03-24: Extended that new compatibility-surface telemetry one step further so it now matches the documented migration policy. The first slice only surfaced Perl-shaped pass-through syntax; the metadata now also counts older helper wrappers such as `return_a` / `return_m` / `return_ma`, `return_imatch`, `return_array`, `capture_if`, and raw call-wrapper forms. That keeps “ready but still legacy-helper-shaped” rules visible too, without changing the meaning of `language_agnostic_action_ir_ready`.
- 2026-03-24: Pivoted the active ActionIR migration telemetry from “only hard blockers” to a two-lane model. `language_agnostic_action_ir_ready` still means zero RAW_PERL fallback and zero unresolved helpers, but per-rule `action_rewriter` metadata and top-level `action_rewriter_migration` summary now also surface compatibility-shaped Perl syntax separately (`compatibility_surface_*`). That keeps ready-but-still-legacy rules visible instead of letting them disappear behind a green readiness flag, which aligns with the roadmap policy that remaining Perl-shaped compatibility syntax should be flagged loudly until it is migrated to canonical method-like DSL forms.
- 2026-03-27: Continued the remaining Backbone Item 3 owner-contract cleanup in `LinkedSpec::ActionIR::ScannerCore`. The scanner core still had one duplicated internal contract even after the broader owner-dispatch work: the scanner-rule family package list and the shared rebinding symbol list were effectively hardwired twice, once for lazy dispatcher loading and again for dependency rebinding. `ScannerCore.pm` now centralizes those through explicit helper tables, which keeps the scanner-family contract easier to audit and extend without changing scanning behavior. Focused regression coverage now locks that registry-driven architecture seam in addition to the existing lazy-load and scanned-event behavior.
- 2026-03-27: Continued the same Backbone Item 3 scanner owner-contract cleanup one step further. After centralizing the scanner-rule family registry and rebinding symbols, the scanner side still described the dependency contract twice: `Scanner.pm` assembled callbacks from a manual dep-name list while `ScannerCore.pm` rebound those callbacks from a separate manual binding map. `ScannerCore.pm` now owns one `_scanner_dep_specs()` table, and `Scanner.pm::default_deps_for_package(...)` now builds its dep map from that shared contract. This keeps scanner dependency assembly and scanner-rule rebinding aligned under one owner path instead of two parallel lists.
- 2026-03-28: Continued the same Phase 5 runtime-context diagnostics line. Shared runtime-context preparation already cleared stale `top_rule`, but that meant earlier compiler/parser-factory failures could lose the caller’s requested parser entrypoint even when `top_rule => 'RuleName'` was supplied up front. `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` now reseeds an explicit requested `top_rule` during both inline `run_get(...)` and file-oriented `get_parser(...)` preparation, which keeps early structured `last_error` payloads aligned with the intended parser entrypoint without waiting for later final top-rule selection.
- 2026-03-28: Continued the Phase 4 anonymous capture-boundary line with a bounded live-spec spend rather than another new helper. `specs/tkgui.spec::sub_gui`, `specs/hlink_substitution.spec::{substitute_statement2,curlyb}`, and the obvious delimiter-reader return/debug bands in `specs/simenv.spec` now use `capture_slice()` directly instead of spelling raw anonymous-boundary `substr($$STRING, $IPOS, $LSPOS - $IPOS - ...)` reads by hand, and focused regression coverage now locks both the preserved `hlink_substitution` AST shapes and those live source migrations.
- 2026-03-30: Continued the Phase 4 helper-adoption line with another bounded `vhdl` live-spec spend. `specs/vhdl.spec::{entity_declaration,architecture_body,component_instantiation_statement}` no longer use raw `map {lc} @IMATCH_LIST` return expressions. They now snapshot `entry_groups()`, normalize those header items through `lowercase_each(...)`, and rebuild the earlier tagged/array-return AST shapes explicitly. The important migration rule to keep in mind is that replacing a `return_a(...)`-style raw list transform is not just a text-read migration: the explicit replacement must preserve the tagged return payload and current rule array shape too, not only the lowered immediate groups.
- 2026-03-30: Continued the same `vhdl` helper-adoption line with one more small helper-return spend. `specs/vhdl.spec::process_statement` now uses `flat_array(entry_groups())` instead of `flat_array(IMATCH_LIST)`, but the real point is the migration rule it exercises: when a helper return already carries extra payload pieces, the `entry_groups()` conversion must preserve the whole surrounding return shape, not just the group-list slot. Here that means the returned `process_statement` array values and `process_statement_part` scalar stay exactly where they were while only the immediate-group snapshot surface changes.
- 2026-03-30: Continued the same helper-return rule in `sdce` too. `specs/sdce.spec::get_pinport` now uses `flat_array(entry_groups())` instead of `flat_array(IMATCH_LIST)`, and the migration rule stays the same: swap only the immediate-group snapshot surface while leaving the surrounding helper return payload unchanged. Here that means `array_values(a(pieces))` stays exactly where it was, so the live AST shape remains stable while the immediate-match read becomes explicit.
- 2026-03-31: Continued the same `vhdl` helper-adoption line with the first small reordered-group spend. `specs/vhdl.spec::concurrent_signal_assignment_statement` no longer relies on raw `@IMATCH_LIST[-2, -1, 0]`; it now spells that same immediate-group reorder explicitly as `entry_group(1)` / `entry_group(2)` / `entry_group(0)`. The migration rule to keep in mind is slightly different from the flat `entry_groups()` case: when the old code intentionally reorders capture groups, preserve that exact ordering in the explicit helper reads instead of normalizing everything back to the original regex order.
- 2026-03-31: Continued the real Phase 4 feature line with the missing through-cursor span family. We already had stable reads to the current match edge (`capture_slice()` / `capture_from(name)`) and to end-of-input (`capture_rest()` / `capture_rest_from(name)`), but there was still no first-class way to read from an anonymous capture boundary or named checkpoint through the live parser cursor itself. `capture_slice_until_cursor()` / `capture_slice_until_cursor_len()` and `capture_until_cursor_from(name)` / `capture_until_cursor_len_from(name)` now close that right-edge gap cleanly without forcing rules back through raw `pos $$STRING` plus `substr(...)` math.
- 2026-03-31: Commit-workflow continuity needs one more explicit invariant: `git_message_brief.txt` must end each accepted slice as an untracked local temp file, not as tracked repo content. If it is accidentally committed and amendment is not appropriate for the current workflow, restore the invariant immediately with a focused follow-up commit that removes it from the index while leaving the zero-byte local file in place for the next commit cycle.
- 2026-03-31: Continued the same `vhdl` helper-adoption line with a small declaration-reader migration rule. `specs/vhdl.spec::{constant_declaration,variable_declaration,file_declaration,signal_declaration,configuration_specification}` now replace raw `@IMATCH_LIST` destructuring with explicit `declare(...=entry_group(...))` locals. The key point is to preserve the rest of the block exactly: only the immediate-match snapshot surface changes, while the existing `split /\s*,\s*/` fan-out and returned tagged array shapes stay untouched.
- 2026-03-31: Continued the same `vhdl` helper-adoption line with a small interface-port migration rule. `specs/vhdl.spec::interface_signal_declaration` used one of the last raw immediate-group mutation patterns: seeding `@IMATCH_LIST` from the regex groups, then appending the optional `signal_decl_range` child result with `push @IMATCH_LIST, call(...)`, then returning `[@IMATCH_LIST]`. The explicit replacement keeps the same child-call behavior but moves the mutable aggregate into a named helper array seeded from `entry_groups()` and returned via `array_copy(...)`. The important migration rule here is to preserve the child-append timing and returned list shape while removing direct mutation of the built-in immediate-group storage.
- 2026-04-01: Continued the Phase 5 diagnostics line with a higher-value descriptor-build attribution slice. `Compiler.pm` already normalized `spec_descr` failures into structured `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` payloads, but the payload still lost the active parsed rule label even when the compiler knew exactly which rule entry it was compiling. The compiler now tracks that active parsed rule label while `spec_descr(...)` is building the descriptor and preserves it as `last_error->{rule_label}` on both thrown `compile_spec_entry(...)` failures and non-thrown invalid descriptor tuples. The user guide now teaches that `rule_label` is no longer runtime-only: descriptor-build failures can expose it too when the failing parsed rule entry is still known.
- 2026-04-01: Continued the same Phase 5 diagnostics line one stage later in final descriptor assembly. Default `spec_gdata(...)` work already knows which rule label it is compiling combined regex dependencies for, but that attribution was lost if `_ored_re(...)` died during `compiler_pipeline:build_final_descriptor`. `Compiler.pm` now preserves that active `spec_gdata` rule label into `runtime_ctx->{last_error}{rule_label}` on that failure path too, so the compile-time rule-attribution story now spans both descriptor-entry compilation (`spec_descr`) and default generated-regex assembly (`build_final_descriptor`) whenever the active rule is still known.
- 2026-04-02: Continued the Phase 5 diagnostics attribution line across the last non-throwing compiler validation seam. `validate_gdata_references(...)` used to give `Compiler.pm` only a boolean on false-return failures, which forced `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` back to a generic “returned false” detail and dropped the owning compiled rule even when the validation walk still knew it. The validation seam now accepts an optional failure callback, reports summary/detail/rule_label context for false-return failures, and `Compiler.pm` now preserves that owning-rule attribution on `compiler_pipeline:validate_gdata_references` payloads.
- 2026-04-02: Continued the same Phase 5 diagnostics line one step further on the throw path too. `validate_gdata_references(...)` can now notify the same failure callback before rethrowing a nested `validate_rule_definition(...)` exception, which lets `Compiler.pm` preserve `rule_label` even when `compiler_pipeline:validate_gdata_references` fails by trapped die instead of only by false return. That keeps the generated-descriptor validation seam consistent with the earlier `spec_descr` / `build_final_descriptor` rule-attribution work.
- 2026-04-02: Continued the same Phase 5 seam with a summary-normalization follow-up. Once `validate_gdata_references(...)` started reporting structured failure metadata, `Compiler.pm` was still collapsing the user-facing `summary` back to the generic `Generated parser validation failed` banner. That lost useful specificity even though the validation seam already knew the exact failing rule/reference. The compiler now reuses the validation-provided `summary` on both false-return and nested-throw paths whenever it is available.
- 2026-04-02: Continued the same Phase 5 runtime-owner line with the missing silent-undef symmetry. `Runtime.pm` already normalized raw `run_get_pipeline(...)` dies into a fallback `runtime_owner:run_get_pipeline` payload, but it still had one hole: if the delegated compiler path returned `undef` without writing structured context first, callers could see an unexplained `undef` result with no `last_error`. The runtime owner now treats that seam the same way as the die path, writing the stable fallback detail `run_get_pipeline returned undef without structured runtime context` only when no deeper payload is already present. That also makes the file-oriented path cleaner, because `ParserFactory.pm` now naturally preserves that deeper runtime-owner payload on `get_parser(...)` instead of falling back to a generic parser-factory compile-stage message.
- 2026-04-02: Locked the next adjacent Phase 5 seam directly in parser-factory regression coverage. The runtime-owner silent-undef fix made the public `get_parser(...)` path better, but the lower-level `ParserFactory::run_get_parser(...)` compile seam itself still only had direct tests for thrown `compile_spec(...)` callbacks. We now also lock the two silent-undef cases there: parser-factory writes its own `parser_factory:compile_spec` fallback when `compile_spec(...)` returns `undef` with no deeper payload, and it preserves an already-written deeper runtime-owner payload when `compile_spec(...)` returns `undef` after writing one.
- 2026-04-02: Tightened the same Phase 5 runtime-owner seam after verifying a real false-positive. `Compiler::run_get_pipeline(...)` legitimately returns `undef` on successful `parse_only` and `generate_only` calls, so the first silent-undef fallback pass in `Runtime.pm` was too broad: it started writing `runtime_owner:run_get_pipeline` even when those supported introspection modes succeeded. `Runtime.pm` now skips that fallback on intentional `parse_only` / `generate_only` success paths, and direct regression coverage now locks both branches so future diagnostics work keeps `last_error` failure-only there.
- 2026-04-11: Continued the legacy plugin branch cleanup without changing public plugin behavior. `LinkedSpec::PluginBridge` now routes its local `$@` preservation helper through `LinkedSpec::OwnerDispatch::call_preserving_err(...)`, lazy-loads `PPlugin` through `OwnerDispatch::require_pkg(...)`, and resolves registered plugins through `OwnerDispatch::dispatch_owner_call(...)` into `LinkedSpec::PluginRegistry`. The intent is not to bless dynamic `.plg` loading as future architecture; it is to keep the transition branch on the same wrapper discipline as the rest of the owner tree while the remaining plugin surface is retired or moved into explicit package owners.
- 2026-04-11: Continued that same legacy plugin branch cleanup into `PPlugin` itself. The default `load_plugin_parser` dependency is now the named helper `_load_linkedspec_parser(...)`, which lazy-loads `LinkedSpec::OwnerDispatch` only when invoked and then reaches `LinkedSpec::get_parser('pplugin')` through `OwnerDispatch::dispatch_owner_call(...)`. This removes the local `eval { require LinkedSpec }` branch while preserving the important lazy-load property: requiring `PPlugin` and building default deps still do not load the LinkedSpec facade or OwnerDispatch until the legacy registry actually needs the parser callback.
- 2026-04-11: Tightened the same legacy `PPlugin` cleanup one seam further in registry construction. `_build_plugin_registry(...)` no longer slurps `.plg` files through localized `@ARGV` plus diamond IO, because that was global-state-shaped and made read failures/parser-state handling harder to reason about. The new `_read_plugin_file(...)` helper owns explicit file IO, unreadable files are reported and skipped without invoking the parser, malformed plugin parser failures still report the real parser `$@`, and successful partial builds restore the caller's saved `$@` so stale eval state does not leak through compatibility registry construction.
- 2026-04-11: Continued the same plugin-removal line from legacy runtime internals into shipped `.plg` callers. Direct `PPlugin->get(...)` was still present in repo-owned plugin bodies such as `qcflow.plg`, `stan_omap2430c_backend.plg`, `setup_hold_tmax_tmin.plg`, `tssio.plg`, and the then-existing dynamic `plugin.plg` lookup shim. Those moved to `LinkedSpec::get_plugin(...)`, keeping callback lookup behind the registry-first `PluginBridge` transition seam. The newer 2026-04-11 note above records that `plugin.plg` itself has since been deleted after its remaining caller moved to `LinkedSpec::get_plugin(...)` directly. The regression lock now scans every repo-owned `plugin/*.plg` file for direct `PPlugin->get(...)`, so future resume should treat a new hit as compatibility-surface regression unless it is inside the actual bridge/adapter owner.
